# Q40 / V2-FL-F — RED tenancy draft report

**Disposition: HOLD / report. Live catalog run done (round 3, Opus): two of the three suspected gaps are confirmed on a real V2 database. Never merge with TODOs.** Report first, no fixes, no migrations. Base: `codex/sandbox/v2-fl1`. This is the builder's source inspection, not independent verification or a PostgreSQL result.

## Round 3: live catalog run (Opus repair, 25 Nov 2026)

These results are from a real V2 database. They replace the "not executed" lines in the rounds below.

- **Setup.** Cloud container, fresh embedded V2 stack (PostgreSQL 16.10, Node 24.22.3). Built from this branch: FL-1 tip `63dbee15` plus lane A's draft `3cab50c9`. The existing launcher and full seed loader ran; nothing was edited to make it run.
- **Full V2 seed suite: 37 tests, 34 pass, 0 fail, 3 TODO.** The 3 TODOs are the expected-failure probes below. The same result came on three separate fresh stacks.
- **Discovery tests:** `node --test tools/v2-flf.test.mjs` passed 3 of 3.
- **One test-only repair in `FL-F.checks.mjs`:**
  - The progress positive control chose the shared seeded job, whose work an earlier seed case closes, so it failed with `JOB_WORK_CLOSED`. It now picks a firm-A live job whose latest `closed`/`reopened` work-state event is not `closed`. That is the same rule `app.ch4_require_open` uses.
  - Each cross-firm probe now prints the refusal it saw: the SQLSTATE and the routine's code word, never row data.

### Live catalog numbers
- **294** `app` SECURITY DEFINER signatures are runtime-executable (293 names). That is the same count as the Q35 snapshot, so the snapshot was complete.
- **362** candidate rows in total: the 294, plus 68 nested definers that runtime cannot call directly but that are reachable through `pg_depend` or `prosrc`.
- **Owners of the 294:**

  | Owner | Signatures |
  |---|---|
  | jobguard_migration | 271 |
  | jobguard_binding_owner | 5 |
  | jobguard_session_lookup | 5 |
  | jobguard_meter_owner | 5 |
  | jobguard_command_writer | 4 |
  | jobguard_money_owner | 3 |
  | jobguard_audit_writer | 1 |
- **Overloaded names: 6.** `mon9_elect`, `record_builder_attested_acceptance`, `record_quote_disposition`, `record_simulated_settlement` and `switch_job_live` each have one granted and one ungranted signature. `replace_fee_reservation_book` has both signatures granted.
- **24 granted definers have no static caller path** from the TypeScript roots. They stay in the list as unclear: `ent3b_close_abandoned_visit`, 13 `lp11_*_tenant`/maintenance helpers, `lp6d_outbox_issuer_authority`, `lp6d_share_sink_authorization`, `lp6d_watchdog_live_maintenance`, `lp8b_check_extra_command`, `post_recovery_fee_candidate`, `review_invalidated_recovery_evidence`, `signal_outbox`, `success_fee_production_command`, `xa_escalate`, `xc_cancel_authority_loss`. Most are trigger or worker functions, and pg_depend has no trigger edges, so the relation/trigger pass is still owed.

### Cross-firm probes (firm A and firm B share one tenant and have different firm keys)

| Probe | Firm A positive control | Firm B result | Reading |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.require_current_job_parties(uuid,uuid)` | returns the snapshot | **returns firm A's party snapshot** (TODO, missing rejection) | **gap confirmed** |
| `app.require_watchdog_live(uuid)` | returns, job live | **returns for firm A's live job** (TODO, missing rejection) | **gap confirmed**: firm B can learn that firm A's job is live, and it takes the job lock |
| `app.transition_scope_progress(...)` | revision +1, rolled back, row unchanged | refused `42501 WORK_STATE_FORBIDDEN`, row unchanged | refused on this path; the code word matches the practice-session job-ownership check in 0749 (around lines 3760 to 3769), so it is not a gap as probed |

Firm B's direct `SELECT` of firm A's job and progress returns 0 rows in all cases (FL-1 lock).

### What is still open
- All 362 rows except the four named assessments stay **unclear**. The live run fixes overloads, owners and EXECUTE for every row. It does not classify the paths: relation, view, policy and trigger edges and dynamic SQL still need the per-function inspection.
- The two confirmed gaps need a fix packet from the planner. This report fixes nothing.
- Still owed: the independent check and a Codex review of this report.

## Findings and limits (rounds 1 and 2, source inspection)

Three source-identified gaps have executable expected-failure seed probes. All three entry points are owned by `jobguard_migration` in their originating migrations, retain runtime EXECUTE, and their latest source bodies in 0749 check a protected tenant but do not compare the firm. The 2001 / 2047 restrictive policies apply **TO jobguard_runtime**, not to the definer owner. FORCE RLS still applies to owners; the defect is the missing firm restriction on that owner's permitted tenant path, not a claim that every definer bypasses all RLS. Migration 0765's protected tenant accessor alone does not distinguish firms sharing that tenant.

| Function | V2 caller | Class | Proof written, not executed |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.require_current_job_parties(uuid,uuid)` | workspace quote -> QuoteDocumentRepository / QuoteDeliveryRepository; prevention checks | gap: returns party snapshot selected by tenant/job | `FL-F parties` TODO; firm-A snapshot positive control runs outside TODO |
| `app.require_watchdog_live(uuid)` | workspace watchdog repositories -> watchdog.ts; also nested guards | gap: reveals live state and acquires the foreign job lock by tenant/job | `FL-F lifecycle` TODO; live-job positive control outside TODO |
| `app.transition_scope_progress(uuid,uuid,uuid,integer,character varying,character varying)` | workspace proof -> ProofRepository; JobRepository.transitionProgress | gap: scope-progress update by tenant/job/scope | `FL-F progress` TODO; real function return proves attempted update, always rolled back; separate firm-A positive control |
| `app.job_saved_firm_name(uuid)` | quote and invoice render paths | safe at the inspected source boundary | 0757 lines 46-71 compares `current_firm_profile_key` to `final_check_job_scope`; catalog and behavioural confirmation pending |

The first three are **database capability findings**, not claims that a guessed job ID alone bypasses an HTTP application's earlier visibility check. The source-root inventory deliberately includes all production SQL literals under `apps/api/src`, `packages/db/src`, and `apps/web/app`, a conservative superset of V2. The V2 fallback rewrite exposes the standalone Nest composition; the workspace shares that composition. No physical `apps/web/app/api/v2-backend/` directory exists on this base. Route guards, database-name fences and per-path prechecks still need individual inspection before calling any candidate an HTTP exploit.

The original `/mnt/project-files/jobguard-v2/v2-fl-catalog-stop-2026-11-25.md` path is not mounted. For repair1 the router supplied its unchanged copy at `/Users/benharwood/Claude/Projects/jobguard-v2-codex-night/rules/v2-fl-catalog-stop-2026-11-25.md`. **All 294 snapshot signatures are now reconciled below against the source inventory.** PostgreSQL cannot run in this sandbox: this is reconciliation to the supplied Q35 snapshot, not an independently refreshed catalog. The table below groups names, not installed overloads; it is a source lead list, not an exhaustive verified catalog. Historical/dynamic migrations, DROP/ALTER/retarget operations, view/rule/policy/trigger dependencies, computed SQL and unqualified calls can change the graph. Every unresolved row stays unclear; presence of a firm-related word is never used to grant safe status.

`FL-F.inventory.mjs` reads `pg_proc`, owner/EXECUTE/configuration, `pg_depend` and `prosrc` on the actual seed database. Its seed check prints **all** runtime-executable app definers, plus nested candidates (including ungranted helpers and all overloads). Thus a grant with no discovered source path remains visible as unclear. Prosrc matching conservatively includes unqualified calls and cycles; it does not establish path feasibility. Relation/policy/trigger expansion and dynamic SQL require the remaining inspection. No function bodies, row values or credentials are emitted.

## Seed evidence to run on the router's fresh V2 stack

The existing full-suite loader at the end of `tools/v2/seed.integration.test.mjs` discovers `FL-F.checks.mjs`; no shared file was edited. Run the full V2 seed suite with its existing launcher/target checks, not a separate database or fresh login per probe. The suite supplies authenticated firm A/B contexts and its shared sign-in cache. Tests require equal tenants, distinct protected firm keys, firm A's live job/progress, and direct SELECT refusal for firm B. Setup errors fail ordinary tests rather than being absorbed by TODOs.

The progress probe always throws a private sentinel to roll back the transaction after observing the real function's returned incremented revision. Both the positive control and the cross-firm attempt verify the original progress row is unchanged afterwards. This proves only the attempted write inside a transaction; it does not claim an unaudited write can commit. Read probes emit no snapshots or customer data.

Expected-failure assertions demand firm B receive `42501` or `P0002`. An unexpected successful read/write fails that assertion under `test.todo`, exactly as this report packet requests. These TODOs are explicit outstanding defects, never acceptance. They are the packet-specific exception to the normal no-TODO rule; **this draft cannot merge**. No business command, audit writer, authorization, grant, policy or main function changes, so new-command audit/replay/forced-failure evidence is N/A. Existing commands and audit tests are unchanged.
